### BOOK THREE

Although these words are quite meager, yet gather from the magnitude of my goodwill toward you that this is that name in which miracles are most especially performed, and under whose leadership we ourselves rule over nature and accomplish wondrous things without difficulty; it is a name to which no other power of any name resists. To it, the powers of all other exorcisms yield, as do the strengths of secrets and the energies of the magical arts; the celestial stars obey it, as do the infernal deities, natural elements, nocturnal silences, the Memphite secrets <note>Refers to Egyptian mystery traditions</note>, Thessalian potions <note>A common Renaissance reference to Greek witchcraft and sorcery</note>, Chaldean whispers <note>Babylonian incantations</note>, the dogmas of Zoroaster, and whatever exists anywhere that is held dear by those practitioners—all of it trembles, obeys, fawns upon it, or certainly shudders, flees, and hides itself. 

What need is there for more? That name provides such great power from itself that even at its image or its shadow alone, wonders and great works are performed. For we read of Joshua—who by a figurative custom is also called Jesus, son of Nun <note>original: *Naue*. Joshua and Jesus are the same name in Hebrew: *Yehoshua*</note>—that because he bore only a type of Christ and a certain shadowy figure in his name, he crossed the Jordan, overthrew the kingdoms of enemies, divided the land for the victorious people, commanded the sun and moon, and God obeyed his voice. For him, only this was lacking for the integrity of the true name **ihsuh**: that its final aspiration was turned into another guttural, and the letter **Sin** <note>Reuchlin’s term for the Hebrew letter *Shin* (ש)</note> was transposed after the **Vau** <note>The letter *Vav* (ו)</note>, lest a creature should bear the full name of the Creator. And it is thought that he who was previously called Hosea <note>original: *Hoſee*</note> took this name for himself so that by constant remembrance of such a powerful name, he might perform deeds worthy of memory. 

The wise men of the Hebrews have also passed down to posterity a similar thing concerning Judas, son of the priest Mattathias: that he took this surname **Maccabee** <note>original: *Machabai*</note> primarily because that divine verse of Moses was included in the characters of his name: *Who is like unto thee among the strong ones, O **Tetragrammaton**?* <note>Exodus 15:11. The Hebrew initials of 'Mi Chamocha Ba'elim YHVH' form the word 'Maccabee'</note>. By the persistent remembrance of this, he was continually inflamed and endowed with such great and memorable virtue from heaven that he accomplished such distinguished business. 

Indeed, not only the body of Peter but even his shadow freed all those laid out on beds and mats from their infirmities; and the handkerchiefs and aprons of Paul, taken from his body and placed upon the sick and weak, repelled in that saving name not only every ill health but also evil spirits and their harmful illusions. 

Certainly, this was from no other power unless either they were a memorial of the true name **ihsuh**, or a certain hidden attraction of its virtue, or even the very divinity of the name which is the cross of the gallows to which was fixed the title by the silent decree of the dying one, that is: **Jesus of Nazareth, King of the Jews** <note>original Hebrew: *ihsuh hanozri mælech baiehudim*</note>, or likewise in Greek: <term>Jesus the Nazarene, King of the Jews <note>original: ιη̄ς ὁ ναζωραῖος βασιλεὺς τῶν ἰουδαίων</note></term>. 

For just as the **Pentagrammaton** <term>Pentagrammaton: the five-letter name of God, referring here to Jesus (Y-H-Sh-V-H)</term> is the spiritual figure of Christ—about which the first Epistle to the Corinthians speaks in these words: *No one can say "Lord Jesus" except in the Holy Spirit*—so the cross is the bodily signet of the same Christ. Therefore, just as our life is composed of two parts, the speculative and the active, where one enjoys the company of the other and neither can flourish usefully without the other, so we achieve this whole divine gift of fulfilling our will through the theory of the name **Pentagrammaton** and the practice of the cross. If these are joined together, you will find nothing in this vast circuit of the world more absolute. But if separated, it must be that one stands as a symbol of the other...

This page explains how the addition of the Hebrew letter Shin transforms the four-letter name of God (Tetragrammaton) into the five-letter name of Jesus (Pentagrammaton), fulfilling ancient prophecies.</meta>

# ON THE MIRIFIC WORD
<meta>catchword: which</meta>

which is the name called the Tetragrammaton <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH</note>, through the letter *Shin* <note>original: *Sin*. In Hebrew, the letter Shin represents the "S" or "Sh" sound</note>, that is, **ihsuh**. For with these five letters, that most holy and adorable name **ihsuh** must be written. Our ancestors, abbreviating it with only the first three characters, depicted it as **ihs**, as is correctly shown today in the most ancient manuscripts. This was done in the same way it was once the custom of noble Romans to designate their first names with one, two, or three letters. Thus, following the example of our fathers, we have similarly become accustomed to shortening such a noble word with these three letters, **ihs**, even though the "S" is more a middle letter than the final one. 

In this matter, many distinguished grammarians of our age—ignorant of the foreign Hebrew tongue—thought the "H" was a Latin aspiration or the seventh element of the Greek alphabet, called *eta* [η], because that letter is often turned into our "e." Therefore, they decided in their orthography that this name, worthy of worship, should be written without the aspiration <note>i.e., written as 'Iesus' rather than 'Ihesuh'</note>. They did not notice that Priscian of Caesarea <note>A famous 6th-century Latin grammarian</note> established laws for Latins, not for foreigners. For unless that twin breath follows both after the "I" and after the "V," how will it imitate its own primitive source, the Tetragrammaton? Or how will it be truly affirmed that it is written with five characters? 

For if a final "S" is found in either Greek or Latin speech, it should be judged as belonging to its grammatical declension <note>The way the ending of a word changes based on its role in a sentence</note> rather than the substance of the name itself. This "S" does not remain fixed during declension, and frequently changes into an "n" in Greek or an "m" in Latin. Therefore, there are only five characters by which the most sacred of all names, the Pentagrammaton, is formed. Concerning this, we found this Sibylline Tristich <note>A three-line prophetic poem attributed to the Sibyls</note> among the prophecies themselves:

> Then indeed the son of the great God shall come to men,
> bearing flesh, made like unto mortals on earth.
> Bearing four vowels, and the consonant within it.
<note>original Greek: Δὴ τότε γ’ μεγάλοιο θεοῦ παῖς ἀνθρώποισιν. Ἤξει σαρκοφόρος θνητοῖς ὁμοιούμενος ἐν γῇ. Τέσσαρα φωνήεντα φέ-ρων τὸ δ’ ἄφωνον ἐν αὐτῷ.</note>

This was translated by us from Greek into Latin as: "The very offspring of God the great parent shall come, he will be seen in weary flesh like a mortal. He will therefore bear four vowels, and one sounds together <note>one consonant</note>." 

Now, your patience, which you have kindly maintained in listening to me out of your goodness of character, has caused me to make my discourse longer in its delivery. For this reason, I earnestly ask that you grant me pardon for my immodesty and this freedom—not so much of formal oratory as of simple speaking. For I seem now to have sufficiently, and even more than enough, fulfilled the promises I made to you regarding the "mirific word" in that Pentagrammaton name **ihsuh**, which is the most proper name of the Ruler of all things. He, departing from us, testified these things about himself, saying:

> All power is given to me in heaven and on earth,
<note>Matthew 28:18</note>

so that we may know, according to the Epistle to the Ephesians, what is the exceeding greatness of the power of God toward us who believe, according to the working of his mighty power which he wrought in Christ, when he raised him from the dead and set him at his own right hand in the heavenly places, far above all principality, and power, and might, and dominion, and every name that is named, not only in this world but also in that which is to come, and has put all things under his feet <note>Ephesians 1:19-22</note>. And Paul says to the Philippians that He exalted him and gave him a name which is above every name:

> that at the name of **ihsuh** every knee should bow, of things in heaven, and things in earth, and things under the earth.
<note>Philippians 2:9-10</note>

He was made so much better than the angels, as it stands in the letters sent to the Hebrews, by as much as he has inherited a more excellent name than they <note>Hebrews 1:4</note>. To conclude this burden from all the evidence, both...

### BOOK THREE
->k iii<-

Even if arising from quite slender beginnings, yet proceeding from the greatness of my goodwill toward you, conclude that this is that Name in which miracles primarily occur, and under whose guidance we ourselves rule over nature and perform marvelous things without difficulty—a Name which no other power of any other name can resist. To it, the powers of other adjurations yield, as do the strengths of secrets and the energies of the arts. The stars of heaven, the powers of the underworld, the natural elements, the silence of the night, Memphite mysteries <note>Refers to ancient Egyptian magic centered in Memphis</note>, Thessalian potions <note>Thessaly was legendary in the ancient world for its witches and herbal magic</note>, Chaldean chants, the doctrines of Zoroaster, and whatever else is claimed to be pleasing to those practitioners—all of it trembles, obeys, and fawns before it, or certainly dreads it, flees, and is hidden. What need is there for more words? This Name provides such great power from itself that even at its image or its shadow alone, wonders and great works are performed. 

For we read of Joshua, who is also called by a common custom "Jesus, son of Nun," because he bore only a type of Christ and a certain shadowy figure of the Name. He crossed the Jordan, overthrew the kingdoms of enemies, divided the land for the victorious people, commanded the sun and moon, and God obeyed his voice. To the integrity of the true name **ihsuh** <note>The five-letter name, or Pentagrammaton</note>, he lacked only this: that his final aspiration was turned into another guttural, and the *Shin* <note>The Hebrew letter 'S' added to the Tetragrammaton to form the name of Jesus</note> was transposed after the *Vau* <note>The letter 'V' or 'U'</note>, so that a creature might not bear the full name of the Creator. It is thought that he took this as a surname—having previously been called Hoshea—so that by constant remembrance of such a powerful name, he might perform deeds worthy of record.

Such is also what the wise among the Hebrews passed down to posterity concerning Judas, son of Mattathias the priest: that he took the surname "Maccabee" <note>Judas Maccabeus, leader of the Jewish revolt against the Seleucids</note> primarily for this reason—because that divine verse of Moses was included in the characters of his name: *Who is like unto thee among the strong, O Tetragrammaton?* <note>Exodus 15:11. The Hebrew initials of this phrase—Mi Kamocha Ba-elim YHWH—spell MKBI, or Maccabee</note>. Through the persistent remembrance of this, he was continually set on fire and gifted from heaven with such great and memorable virtue that he accomplished such illustrious tasks. 

Indeed, it was not only Peter's body, but even his shadow that freed all those laid out on beds and mats from their infirmities. And only the handkerchiefs and aprons of Paul, taken from his body and placed upon the sick and the weak in that saving Name, drove away not only every ill health but also wicked spirits and their harmful illusions. Certainly, this happened from no other power except that they were either a memorial of the true name **ihsuh**, or a certain hidden attraction of its virtue, or even the very power of the Name which is the cross of the gallows, to which was fixed the title by the silent decree of the dying man: that is, **ihsuh hanozri mælech baiehudim** <note>Hebrew for "Jesus the Nazarene, King of the Jews"</note>, or likewise in Greek: **ih̃s o ναζωραῖος βασιλεὺς τῶν Ἰουδαίων** <note>original Greek: ih̃s ho Nazōraios basileus tōn Ioudaiōn - "Jesus the Nazarene, King of the Jews"</note>. 

For just as the **Pentagrammaton** <note>The five-letter name</note> is the spiritual figure of Christ, about which the first epistle to the Corinthians discourses in these words: *No one can say "Lord Jesus" except in the Holy Spirit*, so the Cross is the physical seal of the same Christ. Therefore, just as our life is composed of two parts—the speculative and the active—one of which enjoys the company of the other, and neither can thrive usefully without the other, so we achieve this entire divine gift of fulfilling our will through the theory of the Pentagrammaton name and the practice of the Cross. If these are joined together, you will experience nothing more absolute in this vast circuit of the earth. If separated, however, it must be that one stands as the symbol of the other...

### BOOK THREE

Even if these arguments are somewhat meager, conclude from the greatness of my goodwill toward you that this is the very name in which miracles are chiefly performed. Under its guidance, we ourselves master nature, and we carry out wondrous things without difficulty; no other power of any name whatsoever resists it. To this name, the powers of all other incantations yield, as do the strengths of secrets and the energies of the arts. The stars of heaven, the powers of the underworld, the elements of nature, the silences of the night, the mysteries of Memphis <note>original: "arcana Memphitica"; referring to ancient Egyptian magic</note>, the potions of Thessaly <note>original: "Thesſalica pharmaca"; Thessaly was famous in the ancient world for its witches and herbal magic</note>, the murmurs of the Chaldeans, the dogmas of Zoroaster, and whatever else exists anywhere that is considered pleasing to those craftsmen—all of it trembles, obeys, serves as a fawning attendant, or certainly dreads it, flees, and hides.

What need is there for more words? This name provides such power from itself that through even its image or its shadow alone, wonders and great works are performed. For we read of Joshua—who is also called Jesus son of Nun <note>original: "Naue"; the Greek and Latin form of the Hebrew name Nun</note> in a transitional manner—that because he bore a mere "type" of Christ and a certain shadowy figure in his name, he crossed the Jordan, overthrew the kingdoms of enemies, divided the land among the victorious people, commanded the sun and moon, and God obeyed his voice. This was done so that a creature <note>i.e., a human being</note> would not bear the complete name of the Creator. It is thought that he took this as a surname—having previously been called Hoshea—so that through constant remembrance of such a powerful name, he might perform deeds worthy of memory.

Being continually set on fire by the persistent remembrance of this, he was endowed from heaven with such great and memorable virtue that he accomplished such noble business.

And the mere handkerchiefs and aprons taken from the body of Paul and placed upon the sick and weak drove away in that saving name not only every ill health but even wicked spirits and their harmful delusions. <meta>This page continues the argument for the mystical power of the name of Jesus, specifically explaining how the four-letter name of God (Tetragrammaton) was transformed into the five-letter name (Pentagrammaton) through the addition of the letter 'S' (the Hebrew letter Shin), symbolizing the Incarnation.</meta>

# ON THE WONDER-WORKING WORD

...so that one part is brought forth sensibly and openly in a sign, while the other is carried mentally in the intention, with burning faith joining both together. Does it seem a small thing to you to achieve your desires with such great fruit? This is that wonder-working word <note>original: "verbum mirificum"</note> that you have long expected: the portent-making word, the deifying word—nay, rather, the Word who is God and God who is the Word—and the name of the word *IHSUH* <note>Reuchlin’s specific Cabalistic spelling of the name of Jesus (YHSVH), which adds the letter Shin (S) into the center of the holy name of God (YHWH)</note>, and the word of the name *IHSUH*, and the same *IHS* who is both word and name, who is the Lord of lords. This we have learned from Psalm 82 <note>Psalm 83 in modern Bibles</note>, where in the Hebrew it is written thus:

> And let them know that You are Your name, O Lord. <note>original: "Et ſciant quoniam tu es nomen tuum domine"</note>

In place of which we sing differently in the Latin:

> And let it be known that your name is the Lord. <note>original: "Et cognoſcarit quia nomen tibi dominus"</note>

Therefore, the same Son of God made flesh is his very name, *IHSUH*, which is nothing other than the name of the Lord, the Tetragrammaton <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH</note>, except for the addition of a single letter 'S'. This letter allows the divinity of the first syllable to pour into, submerge, and drench the second syllable—that is, the human nature soaked in the "poured out oil"—which is why we showed above that the name and oil are the same thing. For among the Hebrews, both begin with an 'S': *Shem* <note>original: "Sem"</note>, which is "name," and *Shemen* <note>original: "semen"</note>, which is "oil" <note>Reuchlin is drawing a mystical linguistic connection between the Hebrew words for 'name' (שם - Shem) and 'oil' (שמן - Shemen)</note>. By this, the whole name *IHSUH* becomes pronounceable, and it causes the most precious drop of myrrh <note>original: "ſtacten"</note> to flow down upon us drop by drop. If our mind is prepared in the proper way, it penetrates by such anointing, softens by its soothing, and fills us with the most pleasing liquid of divinity. Its anointing is more fluid than the clearest waters, receiving into itself the splendors of all sciences, just as shimmering water or a polished body smeared with oil can catch any ray of light. For this reason, it deserves to be called not only wonder-working but also prophetic. Concerning this, listen to the Epistle of John speaking of Jesus:

> "And the anointing," he says, "which you have received from him, let it remain in you; and you have no need for anyone to teach you, but as his anointing teaches you concerning all things, it is true and is not a lie." <note>1 John 2:27</note>

Considering this, Paul judged himself to know nothing except *IHS* Christ, and him crucified. In vain, therefore, do we flee to soothsayers and the secret priests of the magi, or to Egyptian disciplines, or any other college of superstition, since we ourselves hold a more certain formula of prophecy: the familiar power of a single name, easy ceremonies, unbelievable powers, and wonderful transformations. We carry in our own mouths the dominion of all nature, the command of every secret virtue in heaven and on earth, and the spirit of omnipotence. Of this, Jeremiah speaks in his lamentation:

> The spirit of our mouth is Christ the Lord. <note>Lamentations 4:20</note>

But let us cast away the deadly secrets of incantations, the vain furies, the arts that must be entreated, the love-potions intended to force the gods, Colchian juices <note>A reference to the poisons and magic of Medea, who was from Colchis</note>, and demonic factions. Finally, let us hand over to the *Pyriphlegethon* <note>The river of fire in the Greek underworld</note>—that is, to the fire and the hearth—the Plutonic volumes, the books of shadows, the Stygian conjurations, and the Acherontic rites contained on parchment or paper. The authors themselves from whom this vanity took its origin are consigned to that fire, not without their own everlasting torment. Let us strive toward God, and let us have unshaken, sincere, and simple trust in the Son of God in whom we live, move, and have our being. He promised us with his own mouth of truth, pledging infallibly:

> "Amen, amen I say to you, he who believes in me, the works that I do, he will do also; and greater than these will he do, because I go to the Father. And whatever you ask the Father in my name, this I will do, so that the Father may be glorified in the Son." <note>John 14:12-13</note>

If you ask anything in my na...

<summary>Reuchlin explains that the name of Jesus (IHSUH) is the miracle-working "Pentagrammaton," formed by adding the letter 'S' to the divine name of God. He rejects all traditional magic and pagan sorcery, arguing that true power and knowledge come solely from the "anointing" of this name.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the argument for the miraculous power of the name of Jesus (IHSUH). ### BOOK THREE

k iiii

...in my name, I shall do this. These are the divine testimonies, these are the monuments of our sacred mysteries, these the reminders of our priesthood, these the purest signs, these our ceremonies—content with little, easy to use, and requiring no costly apparatus. This art of ours is higher than heaven, deeper than the abyss, free from dangers, the enemy of shadows, the despiser of ghosts, hating idols, needing neither incense nor unmixed wine, but commanding all ancestral spirits, malevolent ghosts, and phantoms <note>original: "manibus, lemuribus, laruis". These were different classes of spirits or ghosts in Roman belief; the author suggests the Name of Jesus commands them all.</note>. It looks down upon all tombs, all sepulchers, and the appearances of the dead; with a cheerful countenance, it drives away empty terrors, night-time encounters, and the paths to the underworld, overcoming both fate and nature. And whatever we may ever hope for, if the method handed down by the master is observed, it brings it to pass without ceasing and fulfills it without fail.

It is told that a man, lame from his mother’s womb and from his cradle, was carried by the hands of his kin in Jerusalem; a poor man, he was brought on a litter so that he might seek a living through begging before the gates of the temple, earnestly imploring the pity of the crowd entering and leaving, because he was unable to stand on his feet. But Peter said to him:

> In the name of Jesus Christ of Nazareth, rise and walk. <note>From Acts 3:6</note>

And leaping up, he stood and walked, so that it might appear how much the name of the Master succors the disciple, and how much imitation of Him profited. The Master healed all illnesses with a word and put all demons to flight; but the disciple also said to the dead Dorcas:

> Tabitha, rise. <note>original: "Tabia". From Acts 9:40. Dorcas/Tabitha was a woman known for her charity whom Peter raised from the dead.</note>

And she returned to life. These things happened in Joppa; listen to what occurred in Lydda: Aeneas, who had been bedridden for eight years as a paralytic, was freed by that same Peter saying:

> Aeneas, the Lord Jesus Christ heals you; rise and make your bed. <note>From Acts 9:34</note>

And immediately he arose. In Macedonia, Paul cast out a spirit of Python <note>A "spirit of Python" refers to a spirit of divination or oracular possession, similar to the Delphic Sibyl.</note> from a demon-possessed girl with these words:

> I command you in the name of Jesus Christ to go out of her. <note>From Acts 16:18</note>

And it exited in that same hour. Furthermore, through the hands of the apostles, many signs and wonders were done among the people, who prayed to God in this way:

> Lord, grant to your servants to speak your word with all confidence, by stretching out your hand to heal, and that signs and wonders may be done through the name of your holy son Jesus. <note>From Acts 4:29-30</note>

And when they had prayed, the place in which they were gathered was shaken. Hermione, the daughter of the apostle Philip, and Petronilla—the disciple whom Paul taught and who professed the art of medicine—healed everyone by the invocation of Jesus Christ. By that same word, the priest Philotheus, son of Theophila, turned a stream into wine, and moved a great rock merely by speaking, and soon provided a sufficiency of bread to the hungry.

<margin>Jerome?</margin>
In the writings of the priest Jerome—who was famous, I believe, for both his abundance of learning and the truth of his scriptures—you have read concerning the life of Hilarion of Palestine. Among many things most worthy of admiration, there is that story of a certain candidate of the Emperor Constantius, born in the Frankish region of Germany, whom a malignant demon had possessed from his tender years <note>original: "a teneris unguiculis", literally "from his tender little nails," a Latin idiom for early childhood.</note>. He was led to Gaza with a huge retinue and honor to see Hilarion, explaining in both the Syrian and Greek languages (which he had never learned) the complex cause of his possession. The holy man answered him:

> I do not care how you entered, but I command that you exit in the name of our Lord Jesus Christ.

So too, having invoked the name of Jesus, he restored three children's bodies to life and health. Almost the entire book is full of wonderful things; consider also in your own minds how much power the town of Epidaurus in Dalmatia felt from the Cross during that greatest of earthquakes, when the receding seas threatened a flood. For when now with a whirlpool of waves and...

# ON THE MIRACULOUS WORD

...by the roar of the waves, and with the mountains broken apart by a mass of stones, it was nearly overturned, **Hilarion** drew three signs of the cross in the sand with his hand extended against the flood; and soon, though it had swollen to an incredible height, the sea gradually sank back into itself before him. I shall remain silent about the power with which he repelled pirates rushing at him, and how he tamed a raging camel by speaking in the **Syriac** tongue, and how, by invoking the name <term>ihsuh</term> <note>original: "ihsuh". Reuchlin uses this five-letter spelling to represent the "Pentagrammaton," a mystical interpretation of the name of Jesus (Y-H-Sh-W-H).</note>, he liberated Orion from a legion of demons who were crying out within him with the diverse voices of a multitude. Indeed, the demons tremble even to this day when the name of Christ is invoked, as **Gregory of Nazianzus** <note>A 4th-century Church Father and theologian.</note> says in his *Defense*; nor has our own malice caused the virtue and power of such a great name to vanish since the time when Christ spoke, as **Mark** testifies at the conclusion of his Gospel:

> In my name they shall cast out demons.

However, those words are not found in the **Greek** codices, nor is that entire passage which begins, *“Now when <term>ihsuh</term> rose early on the first day of the week”* until the end. **Jerome** mentions this in his letter to the Gallic woman **Hedibia**, and **Hilary of Poitiers** gives it some support in the tenth book of *On the Trinity*. Furthermore, **Eusebius Pamphili** <note>Eusebius of Caesarea, the "Father of Church History."</note> fixed the end of the Gospel at the point where it is written:

> And they said nothing to anyone, for they were afraid. <note>This refers to the "Shorter Ending" of Mark 16:8.</note>

In **Luke**, however, we read without controversy that the seventy-two returned to <term>ihsm</term> <note>The name "Jesus" in the grammatical accusative case.</note> with joy, saying:

> Lord, even the demons are subject to us in your name.

And he said to them:

> Behold, I have given you the power to tread upon serpents and scorpions, and over all the power of the enemy, and nothing shall hurt you.

Thus **Paul**, on the island of **Malta** <note>original: "Meleta"</note>—which certain faulty Latin books call **Mitylene**—shook a viper that was clinging to his arm into the fire, remaining unharmed. For how could he have reached **Lesbos** while sailing from **Cnidus** toward **Syracuse**? If you will listen not unwillingly, I shall enumerate for you the locations of his pilgrimage, so that it may be made more manifest that the island was not Mitylene, but Malta, in and around which Paul showed such great miracles on land and sea. 

From **Damascus** he went to **Jerusalem**, from there to **Tarsus**, then to **Antioch**, again to Jerusalem, and a second time to Antioch. There, set apart for the apostolate with **Barnabas**, he reached **Seleucia**, then **Cyprus** (where he began to be called Paul), afterward **Perga**, and **Antioch in Pisidia**, and **Iconium**, and **Lystra**, and **Derbe in Lycaonia**, and **Pamphylia**, and again **Perga** and **Attalia**. Then, for a third time, he went to **Antioch in Syria**, and a third time to Jerusalem because of the dispute over circumcision. Again, a fourth time to Antioch; then he passed through **Syria** and **Cilicia**, reaching Derbe and Lystra a second time, where he circumcised **Timothy**. 

Afterward he entered **Phrygia**, and the lands of the **Galatians**, **Mysia**, **Troas**, **Neapolis**, and **Philippi in Macedonia**, **Amphipolis**, **Apollonia**, **Thessalonica**, **Berea**, **Athens**, **Corinth**, **Ephesus**, **Caesarea**, and a second time to Antioch in Pisidia, and a second time to Ephesus. From there, traveling through Macedonia, he returned a second time to the **Philippians**, and again to the region of **Troy** where he raised **Eutychus** from the dead. From there he came to **Assos**, then to **Mitylene**, and toward **Chios**, and to **Samos**, **Miletus**, **Cos**, **Rhodes**, **Patara**, **Tyre**, **Ptolemais**, **Caesarea**, and for a fourth time to Jerusalem. From there he was sent to Caesarea and, having been bound, finally to **Rome**. 

In this manner, from Caesarea he indeed put in at **Sidon**, then **Myra in Lycia**, then **Cnidus**, and at last to the island which is called **Malta**, where he cast off the serpent clinging to his body without harm. From that place he was led to **Syracuse**, then **Rhegium**, then **Puteoli**, and finally by foot...

In the progress toward **Rome**, so that through constant travel far and wide, the recognition of such a great name might become ever more renowned among various nations; for all would see that in this one name alone, such great divinity had been granted to men and such power entrusted for performing wondrous things. Thus **Sylvester** <note>Pope Sylvester I (r. 314–335), who according to medieval legend bound a dragon that lived in a cave beneath the Capitoline Hill.</note> shut up the dragon in the Capitol. And **Philip** put the **Leviathan** to flight in the name of <term>ihsuh</term> <note>Reuchlin uses the "Pentagrammaton" spelling IHSUH to represent the name of Jesus, which he believes contains the power of the four-letter name of God plus the letter 'S' for the Word made flesh.</note>. **John the Evangelist**,
<margin>John the Evangelist</margin>
having overcome poison, expelled a demon from the temple of **Diana** <note>The Temple of Artemis (Diana) at Ephesus, one of the Seven Wonders of the Ancient World.</note> who had resided there for two hundred and forty-nine years, casting him from the place with these words: "I forbid you in the name of **Jesus Christ of Nazareth** <note>original Latin: "ihsuh christi nazareni"</note> to dwell here any longer," and immediately the demon departed from **Ephesus**. Because of this, by order of the Emperor **Domitian**, the holy man was deported into exile on the island of **Patmos**, along with **Prochorus** his companion, where he performed such great miracles through the name of <term>ihsuh</term> that the priests of **Apollo**, struck by both resentment and the fear of being abandoned by their followers, reported the whole matter with screeching complaints to **Cynops**, the chief of magicians, who lived in a certain deserted place near **Phora**, a town on that island.

**Cynops**, to relieve them of this trouble, promised to send evil angels to **John** to kill him. And when he had sent one of the malignant spirits to **Phora**, to whom he had assigned the cruel task of murder, and as it was already gliding into the house of **Myron** where **John** was, the Apostle, foreseeing the cause, said to the demon: "I forbid you in the name of **Jesus Christ** <note>original Latin: "ihsuh christi"</note> to move from this place until you first openly confess for whose sake you have come." And at the spoken word, the spirit stood still, as if bound by a chain. And again **John** said: "Come, tell me why you have approached this house." The spirit replied: "Because the priests of **Apollo** met with **Cynops** with wailing, preaching many evils about you. Since they were urging him to come into the city to kill you, **Cynops** said he had lived in that wilderness for many years and certainly had never entered the city even for a greater cause; therefore, he felt it was beneath him to go now for the sake of this little man <note>The Latin *homuncio* is a diminutive used here as a term of contempt by the magician.</note>. Thus, instructing me as I was about to go, he ordered me to bring your soul to him, so that he might prepare to hand it over to judgment."

But **John** replied: "Have you ever been sent by him before to take someone's soul?" And the spirit answered: "I have indeed been sent, and I have killed as well, but I have never carried away a soul." Then **John** said: "Why do you obey him?" And the spirit, responding to this, said: "All the power of **Satan** dwells in that man, and he has entered into a conspiracy with all the principalities, and we ourselves likewise with him; and **Cynops** obeys us, and we in turn obey him." And **John** said: "Listen, malignant spirit, **John** the Apostle of the son of God, **Jesus Christ**, commands you never again to go forth to ambush a human being, nor to depart from this place." 

Because that spirit did not return to the Magician, **Cynops** sent another who met with a similar fate. He sent again two of the princes of spirits, one of whom entered the house while the other stood outside to watch what would happen. But the one who entered remained by the same command, while the one who stood at the doors returned quickly to announce the event to the Magician. At this, **Cynops**, inflamed with fury and taking up his entire cohort of demons, went into the city; and the whole city witnessed and applauded **Cynops**, and they all worshipped him. But when he heard that **John** was teaching publicly, he was filled with the burning smoke of wrath and said to the people: "O blind men, wandering from the truth..."

...listen to the way: if John is honest and his words are right, he will care for both you and me through the word I speak to him. I too will believe in his words and deeds." And grabbing a certain young man, he asked whether his father was still living. When the young man replied that he was dead, he asked, "By what death?" To which he answered, "Because he was a sailor and once perished in the sea, suffocated by the force of the waves."

Then **Cynops**, turning toward **John**, said: "Now, show whether the things you preach are true; bring back this young man’s father from the sea, and restore him alive to this boy and to all of us." **John** answered him: "Christ did not send me to raise the dead, but to teach those who go astray." Then the **Magician**, boasting before the common people, spoke these words: "Now, now you see, men of **Phoris** <note>The "Phorenses" likely refers to the inhabitants of the harbor or marketplace (from the Greek 'phora') on Patmos where the crowd had gathered.</note>, what a deceiver this man is who misleads you with magical illusions. Seize and hold him until I restore the father alive to the youth." And so they held **John**.

**Cynops**, however, with palms outstretched toward the shores of the sea, stomping his feet, made a sound like a great crash in the sea, and thus disappeared from their eyes. Therefore all those standing by cried out with a raised voice: "Great are you, **Cynops**, and there is none besides you!" Soon the **Magician** rose from the waters having—as he himself said—the boy's parent, and all were astonished in amazement. And he said to the boy, "Is this your father?" He replied, "Certainly, lord, it is he." And everyone began to worship **Cynops**, and they resolved to kill **John**, which the **Magician** forbade, saying: "After you have seen much greater things than these, then finally afflict him most severely."

And calling to himself another man, he asked: "Do you have a son?" But he said: "Certainly, lord, I had one, but he was killed by slaughter and treachery." The **Magician** said to him: "Your son shall live again," and raising his voice, he called by name both the murderer and the one who had been killed. And immediately both stood there together. **Cynops** said to that man: "Is this your son, and is this the one who killed him?" The man replied: "Yes, certainly, lord." And gazing at **John**, the **Magician** said: "Why do you wonder?" To which he replied: "I do not wonder at these things at all." And again the **Magician** said: "You shall see greater things than these, and then you will marvel." To whom **John** spoke thus: "Surely your signs shall soon be dissolved." Hearing this speech, the crowd so crushed the holy man that he fell as if dead.

But **Cynops**, thinking him already dead, cried out: "Do not bury him under the earth, so that the birds of the heaven may devour him; then let us see if his Christ will resuscitate him." Therefore, each firmly believing him to be dead, they went away together with **Cynops**, praising and glorifying him most highly.

When, however, the **Magician** heard that **John** had gone to a place in the city called **Lithobolus** <note>original: "Lithobolus." From the Greek for "stony" or "thrown with stones," this is the traditional site of the confrontation in the apocryphal Acts of John.</note>, to preach and teach there, he approached again with his demons, by whose service he practiced <term>Necromancy</term> <note>The magical art of conjuring the spirits of the dead, which Reuchlin views here as a demonic deception rather than true resurrection.</note>, not without a large crowd of following men, among whom these three "resurrected" ones also walked—who, though they were demons, were thought to be men. And the **Magician** said to **John**: "Behold, I forbade you to be killed so that I might strike you with greater shame and fill your face with ignominy. Therefore, come, if you please, to the harbor so that you may behold my power, and you will marvel further." Therefore **John** returned with the whole multitude, commanding those three demons who were accompanying them under the likeness of the recently risen men...

...lest they should depart. And so, with hands struck together and the terrible sound of the sea having been produced, **Cynops** lowered himself into the sea as before, and thus vanished from the eyes of all, who did not cease to cry out in praise, saying, "Great are you, **Cynops**, and there is no one besides you." **John**, meanwhile, was praying to the Lord that this Magician should no longer be counted among the living. Then suddenly a violent murmur of the sea was heard, and as storms rushed into that place where he had cast himself, he appeared nowhere. And the demons who stood in human form, as if long ago raised from the dead, were adjured by the Apostle of God, who said: "In the name of the crucified **Jesus Christ**, leave this island, never to return." And immediately they vanished from the eyes of all who were waiting for **Cynops**, to see if he might perhaps rise again from the strait. And when they had remained there for three days and three nights, many of them, weakened by hunger, noise, and heat, lay falling on the ground like mutes. From among them, three youths died, whom **John**, taking pity, finally raised, preaching to the people those things which lead to salvation. They, approaching **Christ**, were baptized, leaving the Magician like a second **Pharaoh** in the depths of the sea.

<margin><image-desc>A flourished pen mark, possibly a personal mark or a nota bene symbol.</image-desc></margin>

I could recite many more things to you about that holy Evangelist, and what and how great powers of our most sacred and miraculous name he showed both on earth and on the high sea, and in what way, after the death of the Emperor **Domitian**, he returned from the island and raised his and the saints' hostess **Drusiana** <note>A prominent follower of John in Ephesus according to the apocryphal Acts of John.</note> from the dead with this voice alone, saying: "**Jesus Christ** rouse you, **Drusiana**; rise and go home to prepare dinner for me." So too did the Apostle **Thomas** raise a woman long dead. And in the name of **Jesus**, **Bartholomew** shattered the image of the sun and visibly expelled a demon from it. But the whole day would fail me, even one newly begun, and you would perhaps be affected by weariness from such a long discourse. Let it be enough to have proposed these things only, so that you may understand that just as the Father is in the Son, and the Son in men, so the name of the Father is glorified through the Son, and the name of the Son is glorified through men; and the name of the Son and the Father is one, except for a consonant letter, just as the Son and Father are one, except for the nature of humanity. For this reason he truly said, "O Father, I have manifested your name to men, because the words which you gave to me I have given to them, that they may be one just as we are one. I in them and you in me, that they may be perfected into one; and I have made known your name to them and will make it known, so that the love with which you have loved me may be in them, and I in them." You are no longer ignorant, since you have heard the perfect doctrine of every stupendous operation and of all wonderful things. This is the place of that art, this the foundation, this the discipline; here is the <term>philosophers' stone</term> <note>original: "lapis philosophorum"; here used metaphorically for the divine Name rather than a physical alchemical substance</note>, far surpassing that over which the Alchemists <note>original: "Archimici," a variant of Alchimici</note> errantly contend. This is the voice. These are the characters by which omnipotence is invoked for things to be done beyond the common mode of life. Of which **Zechariah** says: "He himself shall call upon my name, and I will hear him." By three characters in the time of nature, and four characters in the time of law, and five characters in the time of grace <note>The author is likely referring to the progression from the three-letter name (divine essence), to the four-letter Tetragrammaton (YHWH), to the five-letter Pentagrammaton (YHSVH/Jesus) used in Renaissance Christian Kabbalah.</note>, the divine omnipotence was invoked. For the Lord spoke to **Moses**, saying: "I am the <term>Tetragrammaton</term> <note>original: "Tetragrammus"; the four-letter Name of God</note> who appeared to **Abraham**, **Isaac**, and **Jacob** in God **Shaddai** <note>original: "Sadai"; an ancient Hebrew name for God, often translated as "Almighty"</note>, and my name **Adonai**—"

# ON THE MIRACULOUS WORD

...[Adonai], I did not make known to them. And it follows: *And I will take you to myself for a people, and I will be your God, and you shall know that I am* **Adonai** <note>original: "Adonai" - Hebrew for "The Lord"</note>. You have heard **Moses**; hear now the Evangelist and Apostle of God. *This*, he says, *is the command of God, that we believe in the name of his son,* **IHSVH Christ** <note>Reuchlin uses the spelling "IHSVH" or "ihsuh" to represent the "Pentagrammaton," a five-lettered holy name created by inserting the Hebrew letter Shin into the four-letter name of God (YHVH)</note>. Join all these things together, and you will recognize easily that the most powerful virtue of all and the most effective operation has appeared everywhere:

<margin>121</margin>
through the name of the ancestors, the **Trigrammaton** <note>The three-letter name</note>; and of the fathers, the **Tetragrammaton** <note>The four-letter name</note>; and of the sons, the **Pentagrammaton** <note>The five-letter name</note>. That is: in nature, **S. D. I.** <note>Shaddai - Almighty</note>. In the law, **A. D. N. I.** <note>Adonai - Lord</note>. In love, **IHSVH**. For now we are the sons of God; therefore, we use five letters because of him in whom we believe, and whom we invoke as the mediator between God and man, being himself both God and man, the arm and power of the most high God. **Baruchias** <note>A character in this dialogue representing Jewish mystical tradition</note> revealed enough and more than enough to us yesterday regarding this five-fold number.

If you wish, therefore, to perform all things safely, I advise you—not without reason, as **Paul** advised the Colossians: *Whatever you do in word and in deed, do it all in the name of our Lord* **IHSVH Christ**. This is health-bringing; this is the medicine; rather, this is our salvation and healing.
<margin>]</margin>
Thus **Peter** spoke to the leaders of the people and the elders of Israel concerning the man restored to health: *Let it be known to all of you and to all the people of Israel, that in the name of our Lord* **IHSVH Christ** *of Nazareth, whom you crucified, whom God raised from the dead, by him this man stands before you healthy.* For there is no other name under heaven given to men in which we must be saved. This is the courtyard, this the portico, this the entrance, this the first door to God who commanded, *Be sanctified, be clean.* This is the bath by which we are cleansed of filth before God. As **Paul** points out to the Corinthians: *You were washed, you were sanctified, and you were justified in the name of our Lord* **IHSVH Christ**. And that disciple [John] says: *I write to you, little children, because your sins are forgiven on account of his name.*

This is our adoption. For God gave us the power to become sons of God, we who believe in his name. For so it is placed in the Greek <note>original: "εἰς τὸ ὄνομα" (eis to onoma) - literally 'into the name,' implying a deeper union than just 'in' the name</note>, and not as it is commonly read, "In his name." This is our reward. For whoever gives a drink of water in that name will not lose his reward. And everyone who has left home or brothers, or sisters, or father, or mother, or wife, or children, or fields on account of that name, will receive a hundredfold and will possess eternal life. This is the fulfillment of our will. For **Christ** promised, saying: *Whatever you ask the Father in my name, he will give it to you.* This is our consolation. For the **Paraclete** <term>Paraclete: A Greek title for the Holy Spirit, meaning 'advocate' or 'helper'</term>, that is, the Comforter and Encourager, the Holy Spirit, is sent by the Father to us mortals in the name of **IHSVH**. This is our life, as the Evangelist writes: *That you may know that you have eternal life in the name of his son.*

This is our religion, this our reverence: that at the name of **IHSVH** every knee should bow, of those in heaven, on earth, and under the earth. This is the vengeance and retribution against the wicked, who are condemned immediately because they do not believe in the name of the only-begotten Son. This is perfect joy for the pious: *Until now you have not asked for anything in that name; ask, that your joy may be full.* This is a vehement and powerful adjuration and exorcism: *I beseech you, brothers, through the name of our Lord* **IHSVH Christ**. This is our boldness, this our confidence. For **Paul** in Damascus did all things confidently in the name of **IHSVH**, and returned...

<meta>The catchword "cum" indicates the next page begins with "with."</meta>

<summary>This page explains Reuchlin's kabbalistic theory of the progression of God's names from the three-letter Shaddai to the four-letter Adonai, culminating in the miraculous five-letter name IHSVH (Jesus). The text uses numerous New Testament citations to argue that all spiritual power, salvation, and prayer must operate through this specific Name.</summary>
<keywords>Pentagrammaton, IHSVH, Trigrammaton, Tetragrammaton, Adonai, Shaddai, Christology, Kabbalah, salvation, spiritual healing</keywords>

--- Page 176 ---
<meta>This page continues the speaker's argument that the name of Jesus (the Pentagrammaton) must be used in conjunction with the sign of the cross to be effective. It transitions from the history of divine names to the specific "logic" or "reason" of the cross as a mystical tool.</meta>

# BOOK THREE

<margin>81</margin>
...entering and leaving Jerusalem with his disciples, acting faithfully in that name. Why say more? This is the presence of God who says: *Wherever two or three are gathered in my name, there I am in the midst of them.* But believe me, Christ is not without the cross. His cross is our victory. And **Moses**, with hands raised, prefigured the cross and **Amalek** was defeated <note>In Exodus 17, Moses had to keep his arms raised—resembling a cross—for the Israelites to defeat the Amalekites</note>. And in the book of **Ezekiel**, the **Tau cross**

<image-desc>A small marginal drawing of a Tau cross (a T-shaped cross) standing on a three-tiered rectangular base.</image-desc>

saved those who were marked with it <note>The "Tau" is the last letter of the Hebrew alphabet; in Ezekiel 9:4, a mark—historically interpreted as a T-shaped cross—was placed on the foreheads of the righteous</note>. Although the cross among the most ancient—as it is written in a certain collection of the Arabs—is an old symbol of the Magi, because we read that none of its virtues are effective without the name of **Jesus** <note>original: "ihsu"</note>, we therefore most rightly seek and find both Jesus with the cross and the cross with Jesus, by the testimony of Bishop **Martin** <note>Likely St. Martin of Tours, known for his devotion to the cross</note>. This is the "wood" of the Son of Man which will be seen in heaven. This is the second wood of paradise, the tree of life, wood sweet to eat, and beautiful to the eyes and delightful to look upon—not forbidden but offered: *On whatever day you eat from it, your eyes will be opened and you will be like gods.*

This is the miraculous wood that the Lord showed to **Moses**, through which he turned bitter waters into sweetness. For so it is read: *And the people murmured against Moses, saying, "What shall we drink?" But he cried out to the Lord, who showed him the wood.* What need was there in the desert of Shur and the woody wilderness to "show" a piece of wood, when so many trees, branches, trunks, oaks, and shrubs were ready and at hand?

But in the **Hebrew** text, a word is used in this place which signifies to establish, to demonstrate, to teach, and to set a law <note>The Hebrew root "yarah" means to throw or point out, and is the root of the word "Torah" or law</note>, by which it can be understood that God revealed the power of this cross—which is called by a similar name among the **Hebrews**—to his prophet through a certain secret doctrine. Therefore, scripture does not say, "Take for yourself a piece of wood," but *God showed him the wood*, which could be read as: "The Lord instructed him concerning the cross," or "God demonstrated the cross to him."

This is that face which the royal prophet **David** desired above lilies or roses, chanting this half-line: *Show us your face and we shall be saved.* If either the **Pythagorean** philosophy <note>Referring to the mystical significance of shapes and numbers</note> or the **Cabalistic** traditions of the Hebrews do not displease you, you will find that the statue, the wood, the cross, and "your face" are the same thing, over which you heard David crying out to the Lord.

Therefore, with the cross and not without the cross, all our work will be easy. Those who act otherwise lose their labor and expense; they work in vain, are close to danger, and will suffer the loss of their vanity.

Some of the traveling Jewish exorcists also tried to invoke the name of the Lord **Jesus** <note>original: "ihsu"</note> over those who had evil spirits, saying: *I adjure you by Jesus whom Paul preaches.* But the wicked spirit answered and said to them: *Jesus I know, and Paul I know, but who are you?* And the man in whom the evil demon was leaped upon them, and mastering both, prevailed against them, so that they fled from that house naked and wounded. These things are written in the second book of **Luke** <note>The Acts of the Apostles, traditionally attributed to Luke</note>.

Hence, those not observing the manner of the cross and the law known to the faithful will perform nothing worthy of wonder, but only of punishment.

For the word or the "reason" of the cross is indeed foolishness to those who are perishing, but to those who are saved—that is, to us—it is the power of God. Now, what this very word of the cross is, and what that reason of most learned men is, this is the great or rather the greatest and most secret mystery of the miraculous word. Of which

<summary>This page argues that the power of Jesus's name is inextricably linked to the sign of the cross, using biblical examples like Moses and Ezekiel to show the cross as a "secret doctrine" or "wood of life." It warns that attempting to use the name without the proper "logic" or "manner" of the cross leads to failure, as seen in the story of the Jewish exorcists in Acts.</summary>
<keywords>Cross, Pentagrammaton, Moses, Exorcism, Cabala, Tau Cross, Tree of Life, Secret Doctrine, Acts of the Apostles</keywords>

--- Page 177 ---
<meta>This page concludes the third book of Reuchlin's "De Verbo Mirifico" (On the Wonderful Word). The dialogue between the characters Capnion (Reuchlin), Baruchia (a Jewish mystic), and Sidonius (a pagan philosopher) ends with a final secret transmission of the "Wonderful Word"—the name of Jesus interpreted through the Kabbalah—followed by a traditional list of printing corrections and the publisher's imprint.</meta>

# ON THE WONDERFUL WORD

Dionysius the Areopagite <note>A 5th-century Christian theologian and philosopher whose works on "mystical theology" were highly influential in the Renaissance</note> mentions this briefly in his *Ecclesiastical Hierarchy*. Since it is fitting that these mysterious veils and most secret symbols should not be scattered into the breeze, but rather whispered into the ear, I would like you to come closer, Sidonius, so that I may inspire you with my breath. Do you hold it? And he said: I hold it. Then **CAPNION** <note>Johannes Reuchlin's own Greek-style pseudonym, meaning "smoky," a play on his German name "Rauch" (smoke)</note> said: Be silent, hide it, conceal it, keep it governed, be quiet, and mutter not. And you, **BARUCHIA**, lend your ear also nonetheless. Have you received it correctly? And he said: Indeed, well enough. And to you also, **CAPNION**, I forbid—he says—to bring it forth to the common people. For whatever you ask for in this way shall be done for you. Now therefore I will depart. Farewell in good health, and worship the wonderful word <note>The "Wonderful Word" refers to the Pentagrammaton, the name of Jesus (IHSVH) which Reuchlin argued was the "completed" version of the divine name of God (YHWH)</note> most divinely.

->Errors which have been corrected in passing.<-

Folio 3: *Anchisę*, read *Anchise*. Folio 4: *cuius teste fuerit*, read *testis*, and in the same place *mercennaria*, read *mercenaria*, and in the same place *satyricus*, read *satyrus*. Folio 11: *repre hendere*, read *reprehendere*. Folio 12: let *appręhendere* be read without the diphthong. Folio 14: *uersatilor*, read *uersatilior*; in the same place *quã aliorsum*, read *quem aliorū*. Folio 15: *diuinorũ numerũ* <note>divine number</note>, read *munerũ* <note>gifts</note>. Folio 16: *eisdem confes.*, read *eidẽ cõfef.* <note>abbreviations for "the same confession"</note>; in the same place *grauiter*, read *gnauiter* <note>earnestly</note>. Folio 17: *leget*, for *leges*. Folio 19: *Architę taran-tini*, read *tarentini* <note>Archytas of Tarentum, a Greek philosopher</note>. Folio 20: *ad cõmoda patientiũ*, read *petentiũ*. Folio 21: *nõ per mittet philosophus*, read *philosophis*. Folio 24: *Polęmon ite*, read *item*. Folio 25: *de Babylonis*, read *Babyloniis*. In the same place *asseri contra*, read *asserit contra*; in the same place *Moyses hebręos*, read *hebręus*. Folio 26: *fęuã potestatẽ*, read *tempestatem* <note>storm</note>. Folio 27: *Truduxerint*, read *Traduxerint*; in the same place *Tyrannęo quibusq;*, read *Tyannęo & quibusq;*. Folio 28: *quidã habet*, read *habent*. Folio 29: *esse conuersam*, read *cõuersum*; in the same place *Quid ergo*, read *quid ego*; in the same place *ue sicut*, read *ne sicut*. Folio 30: *retractionẽ*, read *retractationẽ*. Folio 37: *tam gręci quo latini*, read *q̃ latini*. Folio 40: *coaceuatio*, read *coaceruatio*. Folio 42: *ubi pauca*, read *ibi pauca*. Folio 43: *uoluit cū cum*, read *uoluit, cum*. Folio 48: *profecto pulchritudine*, read *profecto & pulchritudine*. For *œdippus*, read *œdipus*. Likewise, the studious reader should correct the letters *q;* <note>an abbreviation for "-que"</note> and *quod* which have been placed indiscriminately.
